Lemma D.22
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{n0}(q^{-n}\beta/z,q^{-n}w/\alpha)\mathcal{B}_{n0}(q^{-n}\beta/z,q^{-n}\beta/w){d^{A}_{0}\over d^{\prime A}_{0}}{d^{\prime R}_{n}\over d^{R}_{0}}{g^{\prime U}_{n}\over(q^{-n}\beta/z)^{n}h^{\prime R}_{n}}{q^{n^{2}}g^{\prime L}_{n}\over h^{\prime R}_{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}={(1/\alpha,1/\beta,w/z,\alpha\beta/zw;q)_{n}\over(\alpha/z,\beta/z,1/w,w/\alpha\beta;q)_{n}},

(D.53)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}_{ni}(q^{-n}\beta/z,q^{-n}w/\alpha)\over\mathcal{B}_{n0}(q^{-n}\beta/z,q^{-n}w/\alpha)}{1\over\mathcal{B}_{i0}(q^{-n}w/\alpha,q^{-n}z/\alpha)}{(q^{-n}w/\alpha)^{i}h^{A}_{i}h^{A}_{i}\over q^{i^{2}}g^{L}_{i}w^{i}g^{U}_{i}}{d^{A}_{i}\over d^{A}_{0}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}={\bigl(\beta/z,w/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z/\alpha,q^{-n+1}w;q\bigr)_{i}\over\bigl(1/\alpha,w/z,q^{-n+1}\beta,q^{-n+1}zw/\alpha\beta;q\bigr)_{i}},

(D.54)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}_{nj}(q^{-n}\beta/z,q^{-n}\beta/w)\over\mathcal{B}_{n0}(q^{-n}\beta/z,q^{-n}\beta/w)}{1\over\mathcal{B}_{j0}(q^{-n}\beta/w,q^{-n}z/\alpha)}{(q^{-n}\beta/w)^{j}h^{\prime A}_{j}w^{j}h^{\prime A}_{j}\over g^{\prime U}_{j}q^{j^{2}}g^{\prime L}_{j}}{d^{\prime A}_{0}\over d^{\prime A}_{j}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}={\bigl(\beta/z,1/w,q^{-n+1}z/\alpha,q^{-n+1}\alpha\beta/w;q\bigr)_{j}\over\bigl(1/\alpha,\alpha\beta/zw,q^{-n+1}\beta,q^{-n+1}z/w;q\bigr)_{j}}.

(D.55)

D.8
Final step of proof with Bailey’s transformation formula

for terminating very-well-poised balanced
\boldsymbol{{}_{10}\phi_{9}}
series

Recall that Bailey’s transformation formula for
terminating very-well-poised balanced
{}_{10}\phi_{9}
series
[
28
, formulas (2.9.4) and (2.9.5)]
reads

\displaystyle{}_{10}W_{9}(a;b,c,d,e,f,g,h;q,q)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={(aq,aq/ef,aq/eg,aq/eh,aq/fg,aq/fh,aq/gh,aq/efgh;q)_{\infty}\over(aq/e,aq/f,aq/g,aq/h,aq/efg,aq/efh,aq/egh,aq/fgh;q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(qa^{2}/bcd;aq/bc,aq/bd,aq/cd,e,f,g,h;q,q\bigr),

(D.56)

where at least one of the parameters
e
,
f
,
g
,
h
is of the form
q^{-n}
,
n=0,1,2,\dots
,
and the balancing condition is satisfied, namely

\displaystyle q^{2}a^{3}=bcdefgh.

(D.57)

In particular, by setting
h=q^{-n}
, we have

\displaystyle{}_{10}W_{9}(a;b,c,d,e,f,g,q^{-n};q,q)

(D.58)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={(aq,aq/ef,aq/eg,aq/fg;q)_{n}\over(aq/e,aq/f,aq/g,aq/efg;q)_{n}}{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(qa^{2}/bcd;aq/bc,aq/bd,aq/cd,e,f,g,q^{-n};q,q\bigr).

Proposition D.23
.

Let
n,i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
i,j\leq n
.
Applying Bailey’s transformation twice,
we have

\displaystyle{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}z/\alpha;q^{-i},q^{-j},q^{-n+i}w/\alpha,q^{-n+j}\beta/w,z/\alpha\beta,qz/\alpha,q^{-n+1}z;q,q\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={(1/\alpha,1/\beta,w/z,\alpha\beta/zw;q)_{n}\over(\alpha/z,\beta/z,1/w,w/\alpha\beta;q)_{n}}{\bigl(\beta/z,w/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z/\alpha,q^{-n+1}w;q\bigr)_{i}\over\bigl(1/\alpha,w/z,q^{-n+1}\beta,q^{-n+1}zw/\alpha\beta;q\bigr)_{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times{\bigl(\beta/z,1/w,q^{-n+1}z/\alpha,q^{-n+1}\alpha\beta/w;q\bigr)_{j}\over\bigl(1/\alpha,\alpha\beta/zw,q^{-n+1}\beta,q^{-n+1}z/w;q\bigr)_{j}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}z/\beta;q^{-n+i},q^{-n+j},q^{-i}\alpha/w,q^{-j}w/\beta,z/\alpha\beta,qz/\beta,q^{-n+1}z;q,q\bigr).

(D.59)

Proof.

Observe that the balancing condition as in (
D.57
) is satisfied

\displaystyle q^{2}\biggl({q^{-n}z\over\alpha}\biggr)^{3}=q^{-i}q^{-j}{q^{-n+i}w\over\alpha}{q^{-n+j}\beta\over w}{z\over\alpha\beta}{qz\over\alpha}{q^{-n+1}z}.

Hence, Bailey’s transformation formula (
D.58
) applies and we have

\displaystyle{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}z/\alpha;q^{-i},q^{-j},q^{-n+i}w/\alpha,q^{-n+j}\beta/w,z/\alpha\beta,qz/\alpha,q^{-n+1}z;q,q\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}z/\alpha;q^{-j},q^{-n+j}\beta/w,qz/\alpha,q^{-n+i}w/\alpha,z/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z,q^{-i};q,q\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={\bigl(q^{-n+1}z/\alpha,q^{-i+1}\alpha\beta/w,q^{-i+n}/w,\beta/z;q\bigr)_{i}\over\bigl(q^{-i+1}z/w,q^{-n+1}\beta,1/\alpha,q^{-i+n}\alpha\beta/zw;q\bigr)_{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}zw/\alpha\beta;qzw/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+j},q^{-j}w/\beta,q^{-n+i}w/\alpha,z/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z,q^{-i};q,q\bigr).

Applying Bailey’s transformation once again, we have

\displaystyle{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}zw/\alpha\beta;qzw/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+j},q^{-j}w/\beta,q^{-n+i}w/\alpha,z/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z,q^{-i};q,q\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}zw/\alpha\beta;q^{-i},q^{-n+i}w/\alpha,qzw/\alpha\beta,q^{-j}w/\beta,z/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z,q^{-n+j};q,q\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}={\bigl(q^{-n+1}zw/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+j+1}\beta,q^{j}/\alpha,w/z;q\bigr)_{n-j}\over\bigl(q^{-n+j+1}z/\alpha,q^{-n+1}w,w/\alpha\beta,q^{j}\beta/z;q\bigr)_{n-j}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times{}_{10}W_{9}\bigl(q^{-n}z/\beta;qz/\beta,q^{-n+i},q^{-i}\alpha/w,q^{-j}w/\beta,z/\alpha\beta,q^{-n+1}z,q^{-n+j};q,q\bigr).

Simplifying the prefactor we have (
D.59
).
∎

Now we are ready to state our proof of Theorem
D.3
, therewith its reformulation Theorem
D.13
.

Proof of Theorem
D.3
.

In view of (
D.44
)–(
D.46
) in Proposition
D.19
, and
(
D.59
) in Proposition
D.23
, we have the equality of matrices

\displaystyle{1\over\mathsf{L}^{1}_{i0}\cdot d^{R}_{0}\cdot\mathsf{U}^{1}_{0j}}\mathsf{L}^{1}\cdot D_{R}\cdot\mathsf{U}^{1}={1\over\mathsf{L}^{1}_{i0}\cdot d^{R}_{0}\cdot\mathsf{U}^{1}_{0j}}D_{A}\cdot\mathsf{U}^{2}\cdot D^{\prime}_{R}\cdot\mathsf{L}^{2}\cdot{D^{\prime}_{A}}^{\!\!-1},

which is equivalent to the commutativity
RD_{2}A=ARD_{2}
.
∎

Appendix E
Truncation by tuning mass parameters

In
[
10
]
, we employed the following orbifolded Nekrasov factor for the affine Laumon space:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}^{(k|N)}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)={\mathsf{N}}^{(k)}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)

(E.1)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1\atop j-i\equiv k\,\,({\rm mod}\,N)}\bigl[uq^{-\mu_{i}+\lambda_{j+1}}\kappa^{-i+j};q\bigr]_{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j+1}}\cdot\prod_{\beta\geq\alpha\geq 1\atop\beta-\alpha\equiv-k-1\,\,({\rm mod}\,N)}\bigl[uq^{\lambda_{\alpha}-\mu_{\beta}}\kappa^{\alpha-\beta-1};q\bigr]_{\mu_{\beta}-\mu_{\beta+1}},

with
[u;q]_{n}:=u^{-n/2}q^{-n(n-1)/4}(u;q)_{n}
.
To work out the tuning condition of mass parameters required for the truncation of the Young diagrams,
it is convenient to recast (
E.1
) to the “Nakajima” form,
which is expressed as a product over the boxes of the Young diagrams
(\lambda,\mu)
.
In the following, we temporally replace
[u;q]_{n}
by
(u;q)_{n}
, since we can neglect the monomial factors for our purpose.

By taking the product over
{\mathsf{N}}^{(0)}
to
{\mathsf{N}}^{(N-1)}
, we can remove the selection rule,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa):={}

\displaystyle\prod_{k=0}^{N-1}{\mathsf{N}}^{(k)}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\!\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\!\bigl(uq^{-\mu_{i}+\lambda_{j+1}}\kappa^{-i+j};q\bigr)_{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j+1}}\cdot\!\prod_{\beta\geq\alpha\geq 1}\!\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{\alpha}-\mu_{\beta}}\kappa^{\alpha-\beta-1};q\bigr)_{\mu_{\beta}-\mu_{\beta+1}}.\!

(E.2)

Namely, we can obtain
{\mathsf{N}}^{(k|N)}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)
by factorizing
the total Nekrasov factor
{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)
according to the power of
\kappa
.
Now we can transform the total Nekrasov factor as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)={}

\displaystyle\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\frac{\bigl(uq^{-\mu_{i}+\lambda_{j+1}}\kappa^{-i+j};q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(uq^{-\mu_{i}+\lambda_{j}}\kappa^{-i+j};q\bigr)_{\infty}}\cdot\prod_{i\geq j\geq 1}\frac{\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{j}-\mu_{i}}\kappa^{j-i-1};q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{j}-\mu_{i+1}}\kappa^{j-i-1};q\bigr)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{j}-\mu_{i}}\kappa^{j-i-1};q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{j}-\mu_{i}}\kappa^{j-i};q\bigr)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{\bigl(u\kappa^{j-i};q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(u\kappa^{j-i-1};q\bigr)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\prod_{(i,j)\in\lambda}\bigl(1-uq^{\lambda_{i}-j}\kappa^{-\mu^{\vee}_{j}+i-1}\bigr)\cdot\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu}\bigl(1-uq^{-\mu_{i}+j-1}\kappa^{\lambda^{\vee}_{j}-i}\bigr).

(E.3)

where we have used the combinatorial identity
(
cf. proposition in
[
11
]
,
(2.12)=(2.9)
with
\kappa=t^{-1}
)

for the last equality.
When one of the partitions is empty as in the case of fundamental matter contributions, the Nekrasov factor is simplified
considerably,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\varnothing}(u|q,\kappa)=\prod_{(i,j)\in\lambda}\bigl(1-uq^{\lambda_{i}-j}\kappa^{i-1}\bigr),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{\varnothing,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu}\bigl(1-uq^{-\mu_{i}+j-1}\kappa^{-i}\bigr).

(E.4)

When
N=2
, the factors from the first rows of
\lambda
and
\mu
are contained in
{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\varnothing}^{(0)}(u|q,\kappa)

and
{\mathsf{N}}_{\varnothing,\mu}^{(1)}(u|q,\kappa)
, respectively, which are explicitly

\prod_{k=0}^{\lambda_{1}-1}\bigl(1-uq^{k}\bigr),\qquad\prod_{k=1}^{\mu_{1}}\bigl(1-uq^{-k}\kappa^{-1}\bigr).

(E.5)

Hence, we can see that if
u=q^{-m}
,
{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\varnothing}^{(0)}(u|q,\kappa)
vanishes for
m\leq\lambda_{1}-1
.
Similarly, if
{u\kappa^{-1}\!=q^{n+1}}
,
{\mathsf{N}}_{\varnothing,\mu}^{(1)}(u|q,\kappa)
vanishes for
n+1\leq\mu_{1}
.

In the localization formula of the partition function,
the numerator of the contribution from a fixed point
\bigl(\lambda^{(1)},\lambda^{(2)}\bigr)
, which is identified
with the matter contribution is explicitly given by

\displaystyle\prod_{i,j=1}^{2}\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|2)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(j)}}(u_{i}/v_{j}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|2)}_{\lambda^{(i)},\varnothing}(v_{i}/w_{j}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad{}=\mathsf{N}^{(0)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(1)}}(u_{1}/v_{1}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(1)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(2)}}(u_{1}/v_{2}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(1)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(1)}}(u_{2}/v_{1}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(0)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(2)}}(u_{2}/v_{2}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times\mathsf{N}^{(0)}_{\lambda^{(1)},\varnothing}(v_{1}/w_{1}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(1)}_{\lambda^{(1)},\varnothing}(v_{1}/w_{2}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(1)}_{\lambda^{(2)},\varnothing}(v_{2}/w_{1}|q,\kappa)\mathsf{N}^{(0)}_{\lambda^{(2)},\varnothing}(v_{2}/w_{2}|q,\kappa).

(E.6)

Let us look at the mass parameter tuning conditions such that
the columns of the Young diagrams of
\lambda^{(1)}
and

\lambda^{(2)}
are at most
m
and
n
, respectively.
We need a zero coming from

\mathsf{N}^{(0)}_{\lambda^{(1)},\varnothing}(v_{1}/w_{1}|q,\kappa)

or
\mathsf{N}^{(1)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(1)}}(u_{2}/v_{1}|q,\kappa)
, namely,

(v_{1}/w_{1})=q^{-m},\qquad(u_{2}/v_{1})\kappa^{-1}=q^{m+1}.

(E.7)

Similarly, we need a zero coming from

\mathsf{N}^{(0)}_{\lambda^{(2)},\varnothing}(v_{2}/w_{2}|q,\kappa)

or
\mathsf{N}^{(1)}_{\varnothing,\lambda^{(2)}}(u_{1}/v_{2}|q,\kappa)
, namely,

(v_{2}/w_{2})=q^{-n},\qquad(u_{1}/v_{2})\kappa^{-1}=q^{n+1}.

(E.8)

Recall that we use the following specialization (see (
4.9
)) in this paper:

u_{1}=\frac{qQ}{d_{3}},\quad u_{2}=\frac{\kappa q}{d_{1}},\qquad v_{1}=1,\quad v_{2}=\frac{Q}{\kappa},\qquad w_{1}=\frac{1}{d_{2}},\quad w_{2}=\frac{Q}{d_{4}\kappa}.

(E.9)

Hence, the above conditions (
E.7
) and (
E.8
) are translated to

d_{2}=q^{-m},\qquad d_{1}=q^{-m},

(E.10)

and

d_{4}=q^{-n},\qquad d_{3}=q^{-n}.

(E.11)

The conditions for
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
are the same and those for
d_{3}
and
d_{4}
are also the same.
Thus, there are four equivalent choices for the truncation of the Young diagrams.
In the main text of the paper, we choose
d_{2}=q^{-m}
and
d_{3}=q^{-n}
.

Appendix F
Affine Laumon space and orbifolded Nekrasov factor

The affine Laumon space is the moduli space of parabolic torsion free sheaves
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Originally in
[
35
,
36
]
,
a partial compactification of the space of quasi-maps from
\mathbb{P}^{1}
to the flag
variety of
{\rm GL}_{N}
was defined. The geometric representation theory of the compactification is
controlled by
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
. There is an action of the
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
-Yangian
on the equivariant cohomology of the Laumon space
[
25
]
. The affine Laumon space is an affine analogue,
where the base manifold is replaced by
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}

and we have an action of the affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
.
In
[
44
]
, it was shown that
U_{\mathsf{v}}\big(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{n}\big)
acts on the equivariant
K
-theory of the affine Laumon space,
which cannot be irrelevant to the present work.
In algebraic geometry such “affine” quasi-maps are described by torsion free sheaves on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}

with a canonical framing at
\{\infty\}\times\{\infty\}
and a parabolic structure on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\{0\}
.

In the context of four-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetric gauge theories
the affine Laumon space was first featured in
[
15
,
16
]
,
where the conjecture on the relation of the Nekrasov partition function and the Seiberg–Witten
prepotential was proved. Then as a generalization of the AGT correspondence for Virasoro and
\mathcal{W}_{N}
conformal blocks,
Alday–Tachikawa
[
6
]
pointed out that we could employ the affine Laumon space to describe
the theory with a surface defect whose classification by the embedding of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
subalgebra into
\mathfrak{sl}_{N}

is exactly the same as that of the parabolic structure on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\{0\}
.
In
[
6
]
, it is conjectured when the defect is of the full type the instanton partition function
gives a conformal block for the
\mathfrak{sl}_{N}
current algebra.
23
23

23

For the codimension two surface defect
of general type the conjecture is that the corresponding chiral algebra is the
\mathcal{W}
algebra obtained
from Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{N}
current algebra, see, for example,
[
33
]
.

It is known that the parabolic sheaves are equally described by the sheaves with the
\mathbb{Z}_{M}
orbifold action

\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\ni(z,w)\to(z,\omega w)
with
\omega^{M}=1
.
Moreover a natural toric action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
induces that on the affine Laumon space.
The fixed points of the toric action are labelled by
N
-tuples of Young diagrams, which allows us
to use the technique of the equivariant localization. In practice the relevant quiver is called
the chain-saw quiver
[
26
]
. In particular, the equivariant character of the Yangian action on the equivariant cohomology
of the affine Laumon space was computed in
[
25
]
. It is straightforward to up-lift the formula in
[
25
]

to the
K
theory version, which we use in the present paper.
On the method of orbifolding for the computation of the instanton partition function with a surface defect,
see, for example,
[
19
,
33
,
45
,
46
,
47
]
.

In
[
17
]
, the relation of the asymptotically free Macdonald polynomials and the geometric representation theory of the Laumon space was clarified.
In an attempt at generalizing this result to an affine version, a formula of the non-stationary Ruijsenaars function
is obtained in
[
52
]
based on the affine screening operators.
The non-stationary Ruijsenaars function proposed in
[
52
]

agrees with the instanton partition function obtained from the equivariant Euler character of the affine Laumon space.
24
24

24

Physically this corresponds to the gauge theory with adjoint hypermultiplet,
sometimes called
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory.
For the relation of the formula in
[
52
]
, which we employ in the present paper,
and the formula of the equivariant character in
[
25
]
, see, for example,
[
12
, Appendix B]
.
See also
[
43
]
on the relations of the geometry of affine Laumon space, intertwines of the quantum toroidal algebra
and associated integrable systems.

As we have seen in Appendix
E
, the total Nekrasov factor

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)=

\displaystyle\prod_{(i,j)\in\lambda}\bigl(1-uq^{\lambda_{i}-j}\kappa^{-\mu^{\vee}_{j}+i-1}\bigr)\cdot\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu}\bigl(1-uq^{-\mu_{i}+j-1}\kappa^{\lambda^{\vee}_{j}-i}\bigr),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{j}-\mu_{i}}\kappa^{j-i-1};q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(uq^{\lambda_{j}-\mu_{i}}\kappa^{j-i};q\bigr)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{\bigl(u\kappa^{j-i};q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(u\kappa^{j-i-1};q\bigr)_{\infty}}

(F.1)

can be factorized into
{\mathsf{N}}^{(k|n)}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)
,
0\leq k\leq n-1
,
according to the power of
\kappa
. The combinatorial identities in
[
11
, (2.8) and (2.13)]
also show
the following “transposed” formula for the total
Nekrasov factor:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,t=\kappa^{-1})=

\displaystyle\prod_{(i,j)\in\mu}\bigl(1-uq^{\lambda_{i}-j}t^{\mu^{\vee}_{j}-i+1}\bigr)\cdot\prod_{(i,j)\in\lambda}\bigl(1-uq^{-\mu_{i}+j-1}t^{-\lambda^{\vee}_{j}+i}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\bigl(ut^{\mu_{i}^{\vee}-\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+1}q^{j-i};t\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(ut^{\mu_{i}^{\vee}-\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+1}q^{j-i-1};t\bigr)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{\bigl(utq^{j-i-1};t\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(utq^{j-i};t\bigr)_{\infty}}.

(F.2)

Compared with (
F.1
), the partitions
\lambda
and
\mu
in the formula (
F
) are
exchanged in the product over the boxes of Young diagram, while the form of each factor is kept intact.
Factorization of (
F
) according to the power of
\kappa
gives the following formula
of the orbifolded Nekrasov factor:

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda\mu}^{(k|n)}(u|q,\kappa)=\prod_{i\leq j}\prod_{\ell=0\atop\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}+\ell\equiv k}^{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}-\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-1}\bigl[uq^{j-i}\kappa^{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}+\ell}\bigr]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathsf{N}_{\lambda\mu}^{(k|n)}(u|q,\kappa) =}{}\times\prod_{i\leq j}\prod_{\ell=0\atop\lambda_{i}^{\vee}-\mu_{j}^{\vee}+\ell\equiv k}^{\mu_{j}^{\vee}-\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}-1}\bigl[uq^{i-j-1}\kappa^{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}-\mu_{j}^{\vee}+\ell}\bigr],

(F.3)

where
[u]=u^{-1/2}-u^{1/2}
.
Remark that usually the Nekrasov factor
(such as
{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda,\mu}(u|q,\kappa)
as above) is expressed in terms of
(u;q)_{n}
. But
we find in the case of the affine Laumon space it is more appropriate to use

[u;q]_{n}=u^{-n/2}q^{-n(n-1)/4}(u;q)_{n}=[u][qu]\cdots\bigl[q^{n-1}u\bigr].

(F.4)

When
n\to\infty
, we have to regularize the monomial factor which connects
[u;q]_{n}
and
(u;q)_{n}
.
One of the ways of the regularization is to define
[u;q]_{\infty}
by

[u;q]_{\infty}:=\frac{(u;q)_{\infty}}{\vartheta_{q^{1/2}}\bigl(-u^{1/2}\bigr)}=\frac{\bigl(u^{1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(-q^{1/2}u^{-1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}},

(F.5)

where
\vartheta_{p}(z):=(z;p)_{\infty}\bigl(pz^{-1};p\bigr)_{\infty}
.
One can check

\displaystyle\frac{[u;q]_{\infty}}{[q^{n}u;q]_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bigl(u^{1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(-q^{1/2}u^{-1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}\frac{\bigl(-q^{(1-n)/2}u^{-1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(q^{n/2}u^{1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\bigl(u^{1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{n}\bigl(-q^{\bigl(1-n\bigr)/2}u^{-1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{n}=[u;q]_{n}.

(F.6)

In particular, we have
[u;q]_{\infty}=\bigl(u^{-1/2}-u^{1/2}\bigr)[qu;q]_{\infty}
.
We also find

\frac{[u;q]_{\infty}}{[q/u;q]_{\infty}}=\frac{\bigl(u^{1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(-q^{1/2}u^{-1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}\frac{\bigl(-u^{1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(q^{1/2}u^{-1/2};q^{1/2}\bigr)_{\infty}}=\frac{(u;q)_{\infty}}{(q/u;q)_{\infty}}.

(F.7)

It is possible to express the result of the selection rule in the orbifolded Nekrasov factor (
F.3
)
in terms of the floor function
\lfloor\bullet\rfloor
.
In the following manipulations, we use the formulas for the floor function,

\biggl\lfloor\frac{\ell}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1=-\biggl\lfloor\frac{-\ell-1}{n}\biggr\rfloor,\qquad\biggl\lfloor\frac{\ell+m}{n}\biggr\rfloor=\biggl\lfloor\frac{\ell}{n}\biggr\rfloor+\biggl\lfloor\frac{(\ell)+m}{n}\biggr\rfloor,\qquad\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z},

(F.8)

where
(\ell)
denotes the residue of an integer
\ell
modulo
n
.

Proposition F.1
.

The orbifolded Nekrasov factor (
F.3
) is given by the following formula:

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda\mu}^{(k|n)}

\displaystyle{}=\prod_{i\leq j}\Bigl[q^{j-i}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+n-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\prod_{i\leq j}\Bigl[q^{i-j-1}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{j}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k+(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k+(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle{}=\prod_{i\leq j}\Bigl[q^{j-i}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\prod_{i\leq j}\Bigl[q^{i-j-1}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{j}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k+(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k+(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor}.

(F.9)

Proof.

Since the selection rule on
\ell
is imposed by
\equiv
(the equality mod
n
),

\mathsf{N}_{\lambda\mu}^{(k|n)}
can be rewritten in terms of
\kappa^{n}
-shifted factorial
(z_{0};\kappa^{n})_{m}
.
Let us check that the initial value
z_{0}
and the number
m
of factors for each shifted factorial agree with those in the formula
(
F.1
). To compute them for the first shifted factorial, let us assume

\mu_{i}^{\vee}=q_{1}n+r_{1},\qquad\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}=q_{2}n+r_{2},\qquad\lambda_{i}^{\vee}=q_{3}n+r_{3},\qquad 0\leq r_{i}\leq n-1.

(F.10)

Namely, we may write, for example,
q_{1}=\big\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\bigr\rfloor
and
r_{1}=\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)
. But in the following
we will use
q_{i}
and
r_{i}
for simplicity of notations.
The selection rule in (
F.3
) tells

\ell\equiv k+r_{1}-r_{2}.

(F.11)

Since
0\leq k,r_{1},r_{2}\leq n-1
, we have
1-n\leq k+r_{1}-r_{2}\leq 2n-2
.
Accordingly, we can write the initial value of
\ell
uniformly as

\ell_{0}=k+r_{1}-r_{2}-n\biggl\lfloor\frac{k+r_{1}-r_{2}}{n}\biggr\rfloor.

(F.12)

Hence, we see

z_{0}=q^{j-i}\kappa^{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}+\ell_{0}}=q^{j-i}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{k+r_{1}-r_{2}}{n}\rfloor}.

(F.13)

Now, let us count the number of factors in the first shifted factorial.
Since the initial value of
\ell
is given by (
F.12
) and the upper bound is
\lambda_{j}^{\vee}-\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-1
,
we see

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle{}=\biggl\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}-\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-1-\ell_{0}}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1

\displaystyle{}=(q_{3}-q_{2})+1+\biggl\lfloor\frac{r_{3}-r_{1}-k-1}{n}\biggr\rfloor+\biggl\lfloor\frac{k+r_{1}-r_{2}}{n}\biggr\rfloor

\displaystyle{}=\biggl\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}-r_{1}-k-1}{n}\biggr\rfloor+\biggl\lfloor\frac{k+r_{1}-\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1

(F.14)

Since

\biggl\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\biggr\rfloor=q_{1},

(F.15)

and

\biggl\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\biggr\rfloor=\begin{cases}q_{2}+1,&0\leq r_{2}-r_{1}-1-k,\\
q_{2},&-n\leq r_{2}-r_{1}-1-k<0,\\
q_{2}-1,&r_{2}-r_{1}-1-k<-n,\end{cases}

(F.16)

the power of
\kappa
is

\begin{cases}\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k+r_{1}-r_{2}+n,&1\leq r_{2}-r_{1}-k,\\
\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k+r_{1}-r_{2},&1-n\leq r_{2}-r_{1}-k<1,\\
\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k+r_{1}-r_{2}-n,&r_{2}-r_{1}-k<1-n.\end{cases}

(F.17)

Comparing this with (
F.12
), we can confirm an agreement.
For the number of factors, what we have to check is

\biggl\lfloor\frac{k+r_{1}-\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1=-\biggl\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}-1-k-\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)}{n}\biggr\rfloor.

(F.18)

But this is a special case of the first formula of (
F.8
). We have checked a complete agreement of the first factorial.

Let us proceed to the second factorial. Concerning the initial condition, we can see the formula for the second is obtained from the first
simply by the replacement,

\bigl(\mu_{i}^{\vee},\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigl(\mu_{j}^{\vee},\lambda_{i}^{\vee}\bigr).

(F.19)

Thus an agreement is proved by appropriate changes of variables. However, counting the number of factors is more involved.
Let us assume

\lambda_{i}^{\vee}=q_{4}n+r_{4},\qquad\mu_{j}^{\vee}=q_{5}n+r_{5},\qquad\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}=q_{6}n+r_{6},\qquad 0\leq r_{i}\leq n-1.

(F.20)

Then a similar computation as before gives the number of factors in the formula (
F.3
)

m^{\prime}=\biggl\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}-\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}-1-\ell_{0}^{\prime}}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1,

(F.21)

where

\ell_{0}^{\prime}=k+r_{5}-r_{4}-n\biggl\lfloor\frac{k+r_{5}-r_{4}}{n}\biggr\rfloor.

(F.22)

Hence,

\displaystyle m^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(q_{5}-q_{6})+1+\biggl\lfloor\frac{r_{4}-r_{6}-k-1}{n}\biggr\rfloor+\biggl\lfloor\frac{k+r_{5}-r_{4}}{n}\biggr\rfloor

\displaystyle=\biggr\lfloor\frac{r_{4}-\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}-k-1}{n}\biggr\rfloor+\biggl\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k-r_{4}}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1.

(F.23)

We note
\bigl(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)=n-r_{4}
for
r_{4}\neq 0
, but
\bigl(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee}\bigr)=-r_{4}=0
for
r_{4}\neq 0
.
But such a difference does not matter, since we take a difference of the floor function.
Hence, we can safely use
(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee})=-r_{4}
and what we have to check is

\biggl\lfloor\frac{r_{4}-\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}-k-1}{n}\biggr\rfloor+1=-\biggl\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k+(-\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\biggr\rfloor,

(F.24)

which follows from the first formula of (
F.8
).
∎

We can also write the formula of Proposition
F.1

in terms of the ratio of the regularized infinite products
[u;q]_{\infty}
defined by (
F.5
).
Using the formulas (
F
) and (
F.8
), we find

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k|n)}(u|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle{}=\frac{\displaystyle{\prod_{j>i\geq 1}}\Bigl[uq^{j-i-1}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{-(\lambda_{j}^{\vee})+k+(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\infty}}{\displaystyle{\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}}\Bigl[uq^{j-i}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{-(\lambda_{j}^{\vee})+k+(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\frac{\displaystyle{\prod_{i\geq j\geq 1}}\Bigl[uq^{j-i-1}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{(\mu_{i}^{\vee})+k-(\lambda_{j}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\infty}}{\displaystyle{\prod_{i>j\geq 1}}\Bigl[uq^{j-i}\kappa^{k+n\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-n\lfloor\frac{(\mu_{i}^{\vee})+k-(\lambda_{j}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\infty}}

\displaystyle{}=\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Bigl[uq^{j-i-1}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k-\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\infty}}{\Bigl[uq^{j-i}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k-\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\infty}}\frac{\bigl[uq^{j-i}\kappa^{k};\kappa^{n}\bigr]_{\infty}}{\bigl[uq^{j-i-1}\kappa^{k};\kappa^{n}\bigr]_{\infty}}.

(F.25)

For later convenience, let us express the orbifolded Nekrasov factor in terms of
t=\kappa^{-n}
-shifted
factorials. We have

\displaystyle{}\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k|n)}(u|q,t)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\Bigl[uq^{j-i}t^{-\frac{k}{n}+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t^{-1}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\Bigl[uq^{i-j-1}t^{-\frac{k}{n}-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t^{-1}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\Bigl[uq^{j-i}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\Bigl[uq^{i-j-1}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\frac{\Bigl[uq^{j-i}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\infty}}{\Bigl[uq^{j-i}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k-(\mu_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}\frac{\Bigl[uq^{i-j-1}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\infty}}{\Bigl[uq^{i-j-1}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}-\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k-(\lambda_{i}^{\vee})}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\infty}},

(F.26)

where we have used

\bigl[u;t^{-1}\bigr]_{n}=\bigl[ut^{-n+1};t\bigr]_{n},\qquad n>0.

(F.27)

Finally, the same manipulation as the last equality of (
F.25
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k|n)}(u|q,t)=\prod_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Bigl[uq^{j-i}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k-\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\infty}}{\Bigl[uq^{j-i-1}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}+\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k-\lambda_{j}^{\vee}}{n}\rfloor};t\Bigr]_{\infty}}\frac{\bigl[uq^{j-i-1}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}};t]_{\infty}}{[uq^{j-i}t^{1-\frac{k}{n}};t\bigr]_{\infty}}.

(F.28)

When one of the partitions is empty,
\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k|n)}
simplifies as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\varnothing}^{(k|n)}(u|q,\kappa)=\frac{\displaystyle{\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}}\bigl[uq^{j-i}\kappa^{k};\kappa^{n}\bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j}^{\vee}+n-1-k}{n}\rfloor}}{\displaystyle{\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}}\bigl[uq^{(j+1)-(i+1)}\kappa^{k};\kappa^{n}\bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{j+1}^{\vee}+n-1-k}{n}\rfloor}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathsf{N}_{\lambda, \varnothing}^{(k|n)} (u |q, \kappa)}{}=\prod_{i\geq 1}\bigl[uq^{i-1}\kappa^{k};\kappa^{n}\bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\lambda_{i}^{\vee}+n-1-k}{n}\rfloor},

(F.29)

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\varnothing,\mu}^{(k|n)}(u|q,\kappa)=\frac{\displaystyle{\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}}\Bigl[uq^{i-j-1}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j}^{\vee}+k}{n}\rfloor}}{\displaystyle{\prod_{j\geq i\geq 1}}\Bigl[uq^{i-(j+1)}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{j+1}^{\vee}+k}{n}\rfloor}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathsf{N}_{\varnothing, \mu}^{(k|n)} (u |q, \kappa)}{}=\prod_{i\geq 1}\Bigl[uq^{-i}\kappa^{k-n\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k}{n}\rfloor};\kappa^{n}\Bigr]_{\lfloor\frac{\mu_{i}^{\vee}+k}{n}\rfloor}.

(F.30)

Appendix G
(Anti-)symmetrization in a factorized form

A basis of the space of cohomology classes for the
N
-tuple Jackson integral is defined by using
the anti-symmetrization of the variables
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{N})
with the weight function
\Delta(q,z)
(see (
3.2
)).
In general, the result of (anti-)symmetrization is expanded in terms of symmetric polynomials (divided by the Vandermonde determinant
for anti-symmetrization). However, from the viewpoint of the correspondence of the Jackson integral and the Bethe vector for quantum integrable system

[
48
]
, what we need is the (anti-)symmetrization that keeps the factorization.
One can rephrase it as a summation over partitions of the index set
[N]:=\{1,2,\dots,N\}
.
We give a proof for the following well-known formulas for completeness.

Proposition G.1
.

Let
I_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup I_{s}
be a partition of
\{1,2,\dots,N\}
with
|I_{a}|=k_{a}\geq 1

and
{\mathcal{A}}
be the anti-symmetrization operator of
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{N})
.
For any functions
f_{a}(x)
,
1\leq a\leq s
, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta(1,z)}}{\mathcal{A}}\Biggl(\prod_{i_{1}=1}^{k_{1}}f_{1}(z_{i_{1}})\prod_{i_{2}=1}^{k_{2}}f_{2}(z_{k_{1}+i_{2}})\cdots\prod_{i_{s}=1}^{k_{s}}f_{s}(z_{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{s-1}+i_{s}})\Delta(q,z)\Biggr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\prod_{a=1}^{s}[k_{a}]_{q^{-1}}!\sum_{I_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup I_{s}}\Biggl\{\prod_{a=1}^{s}\prod_{i_{a}\in I_{a}}f_{a}(z_{i_{a}})\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq s}\prod_{i\in I_{a}}\prod_{j\in I_{b}}{\displaystyle\frac{z_{i}-q^{-1}z_{j}}{z_{i}-z_{j}}}\Biggr\},

(G.1)

where the sum
\sum_{I_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup I_{s}}
on the right-hand side
is taken over the partitions
I_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup I_{s}
.

Proof.

For a permutation
\sigma\in S_{N}
, we define
I_{a}=\{\sigma(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{a-1}+i)\mid 1\leq i\leq k_{a}\}
.
Since

{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta(1,z)}}{\mathcal{A}}(F(z)\Delta(q,z))=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{N}}\sigma\biggl(F(z){\displaystyle\frac{\Delta(q,z)}{\Delta(1,z)}}\biggr),

for any
F(z)=F(z_{1},\dots,z_{N})
, we can recast the left-hand side of (
G.1
) to

\sum_{\sigma\in S_{N}}\Biggl(\prod_{a=1}^{s}\prod_{i\in I_{a}}f_{a}(z_{i}){\displaystyle\frac{\Delta(q,z_{I_{a}})}{\Delta(z_{I_{a}})}}\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq s}\prod_{i\in I_{a}}\prod_{j\in I_{b}}{\displaystyle\frac{z_{i}-q^{-1}z_{j}}{z_{i}-z_{j}}}\Biggr).

Let
S_{I}
be the symmetric group of a set
I
.
Then according to the coset decomposition
S_{N}=\mathop{\sqcup}_{I_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup I_{s}}S_{I_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{I_{s}}
,
we take the sum in the following manner:

\sum_{\sigma\in S_{N}}=\sum_{I_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup I_{s}}\sum_{w_{1}\in S_{I_{1}}}\cdots\sum_{w_{s}\in S_{I_{s}}}.

By applying the formula
[
37
, Section III, equation (1.4)]

\sum_{w_{a}\in S_{I_{a}}}w_{a}\biggl({\displaystyle\frac{\Delta(q,z_{I_{a}})}{\Delta(1,z_{I_{a}})}}\biggr)=[k_{a}]_{q^{-1}}!

for each sum over
S_{I_{a}}
, we obtain the desired result (
G.1
).
∎

Appendix H
Shakirov’s equation as a coupled system

Consider Shakirov’s equation in the form

\displaystyle\psi={\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T_{t,\Lambda}^{-1}T_{qtQ,x}^{-1}\psi,

(H.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}={\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi(qx)\varphi(\frac{\Lambda}{x})}}\mathcal{B}{\displaystyle\frac{\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\bigl(q^{-1}d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}\Lambda\bigr)}{\varphi(-d_{1}x)\varphi(-d_{2}x)\varphi\bigl(-d_{3}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)\varphi\bigl(-d_{4}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}\mathcal{B}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}}{q}x\bigr)\varphi\bigl(d_{3}d_{4}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}.

It has a unique formal series solution normalized as

\psi=1+{\mathcal{O}}\bigl(x,\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)
.
In terms of the affine Laumon partition function
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{AL}}
, the solution is given by

\displaystyle\psi(\Lambda,x)={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{AL}}\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}u_{1},u_{2}\\
v_{1},v_{2}\\
w_{1},w_{2}\end{array}\right|x_{1},\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{x_{1}}\right|q,t\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{AL}}\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{qQ}{d_{3}},\frac{q\kappa}{d_{1}}\\
1,\frac{Q}{\kappa}\\
\frac{1}{d_{2}},\frac{Q}{\kappa d_{4}}\end{array}\right|-\frac{\sqrt{Qd_{1}d_{2}}}{\kappa}x,-\sqrt{\frac{d_{3}d_{4}}{q^{2}Q}}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\right|q,\kappa^{-2}\right).

We will give a coupled form of Shakirov’s equation and its solution.
To do this, we define operators
T
and
K
by
25
25

25

One may use
d_{1}
(or
d_{3}
) instead of
d_{2}
(or
d_{4}
).

\displaystyle T={}

\displaystyle\biggl\{d_{2}\mapsto{\displaystyle\frac{q}{tQd_{2}}},\,d_{4}\mapsto{\displaystyle\frac{qQ}{d_{4}}},\,\Lambda\mapsto{\displaystyle\frac{d_{2}d_{4}\Lambda}{q}},\,x\mapsto-{\displaystyle\frac{d_{2}x}{q}}\biggr\},

\displaystyle K={}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi(qx)\varphi\bigl(\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}\mathcal{B}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi(-d_{1}x)\varphi\bigl(-d_{3}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}.

(H.8)

On the parameters
u_{i}
,
v_{i}
,
w_{i}
,
x_{1}
,
\Lambda_{1}
, the operator
T
acts as

T=\bigg\{w_{i}\mapsto\frac{tQ}{qw_{i}},\,x_{1}\mapsto-\frac{x_{1}}{\sqrt{qtQ}},\,\Lambda_{1}\mapsto\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\sqrt{t}}\bigg\}.

(H.9)

In our previous paper
[
10
]
, we used the renormalized
q
-Borel transformation

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}:=T^{-1}_{(qt^{1/2}Q)^{1/2},x}\mathcal{B}

in writing down the coupled system in order to make the correspondence with the
qq
-Painlevé VI equation clear.
If this is taken into account, the action of
T
on the expansion parameters becomes
x_{1}=x\mapsto-t^{-1/4}x_{1}

and hence
x_{2}=\Lambda_{1}/x_{1}\mapsto-t^{-1/4}x_{2}
. The
T
action is the same for
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
and
coincides with the action of the shift operator

\widetilde{T}_{\mathsf{p},b}
with
\mathsf{p}=t^{1/4}
, which is regarded as a square root of the time evolution
of the parameters
b_{i}
in the
qq
-Painlevé VI equation (see
[
10
, equation (6.3)]
).

Proposition H.1
.

The normalized solution
\psi
of (
H.1
) and its parameter transform
\chi:=T\psi
satisfy the following coupled system of equations:

\psi=gK\chi,\qquad\chi=TgKT\psi,

(H.10)

where

g={\displaystyle\frac{\bigl(\frac{td_{2}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda,d_{1}d_{3}\Lambda;q,t\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(t\Lambda,\frac{td_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda;q,t\bigr)_{\infty}}}.

(H.11)

Conversely the equation (
H.1
) follows from the coupled system (
H.10
).

Proof.

From (
H.1
) and (
H
),
one can verify the following relations:

\displaystyle T^{2}=T_{t,\Lambda}^{-1}T_{qQt,x}^{-1},

(H.12)

\displaystyle TK={\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi(-d_{2}x)\varphi\bigl(-d_{4}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}\mathcal{B}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}}{q}x\bigr)\varphi\bigl(d_{3}d_{4}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}T,

(H.13)

\displaystyle KTKT=K{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi(-d_{2}x)\varphi\bigl(-d_{4}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}\mathcal{B}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}}{q}x\bigr)\varphi\bigl(d_{3}d_{4}\frac{\Lambda}{x}\bigr)}}T^{2}={\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)}}{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T^{2},

(H.14)

\displaystyle KT{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T^{2}=\varphi\biggl(\frac{d_{2}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\biggr)\varphi\biggl(\frac{d_{1}d_{3}}{t}\Lambda\biggr)(KT)^{3}={\displaystyle\frac{\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{2}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{3}}{t}\Lambda\bigr)}{\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)}}{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T^{2}KT.

(H.15)

From (
H.12
), (
H.15
) and (
H.1
), we have

\displaystyle KT\psi=KT{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T^{2}\psi={\displaystyle\frac{\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{2}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{3}}{t}\Lambda\bigr)}{\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)}}{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T^{2}KT\psi.

(H.16)

Noting that

T_{t,\Lambda}^{-1}(g)=\frac{\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{2}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{3}}{t}\Lambda\bigr)}{\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\bigl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\bigr)}g,

we see the function
gKT\psi
satisfies the same equation as (
H.1
) with the same initial condition as
\psi
.
By the uniqueness of the solution, we obtain the first relation
\psi=gKT\psi=gK\chi
.
The second relation is the
T
-transform of the first one.
To check the converse, we note that

g\cdot T\cdot g=\frac{\bigl(\Lambda,\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda;q,t\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(t\Lambda,\frac{td_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda;q,t\bigr)_{\infty}}\cdot T=\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\biggl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\biggr)\cdot T.

(H.17)

Hence,

\psi=g\cdot KT\cdot g\cdot KT\psi=\varphi(\Lambda)\varphi\biggl(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}d_{4}}{q}\Lambda\biggr)\cdot KTKT\psi={\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{S}}}T^{2}\psi.

(H.18)

This completes the proof.
∎
